```
PRE-TABLE BLOCK, #501 pcc2defaultkill PASS-3 REPAIR (table 4) -- written BEFORE the table (mtime against
killtable4_20260905/driver.log).  Repair from the nine-seat pass 3 (_scratchpad/panel_20260905_081718): the Opus seat
MEASURED six clean sweeps of the 36-row instrument in its own clone -- M_WRVAL0 / M_WRDATA0 (the WRITE announce's value:
the mirror of pass 2's M_O1, which the pass-2 repair pinned on the READ arm only -- the #499 shape inside the repair),
M_LASTBYTE (data[len-1]: `wide rd srvd' reads four zero bytes, so its channel could not vary -- the CHANNEL check's own
failure mode), M_RELOAD (disclosed: a race no deterministic row can pin), M_BACKEDLOW / M_LATCHOFF (member 6 and the
latch SET conditioned on OFFSET CLASS -- kimi derived the same store); grok and Fable named the len-2-only re-kill;
codex DERIVED three more: a latched-path READ whose response is zeroed (the mirror of M_O2 on the read channel -- every
rowed read after a preceding access expected 0), and the width label made constant on either arm (no byte row required
`(len 1)').  Class closure #2 in ONE diff: TEN rows, EVERY ONE APPENDED TO AN EXISTING SESSION (zero new sessions).

  - SIBLING PARITY -- the announce has five channels (fate, count, direction, width, value) x two arms (writeflag).  At
    pass 3 nine of the ten cells were rowed; the WRITE arm's VALUE was not.  Now: `wr val text' (len 1) and `wide wr val'
    (len 4) -- the latter also separates idata from data[0] (a byte write cannot).  The RESPONSE channel on the latched
    path: pass 2 rowed the write side (`sameoff val'); now the read side too (`srvd rd latch', `srvd wr latch': a
    nonzero byte re-read after its bit was set by a read and by a write).
  - CLAIM -> ROW -- the contract claimed "a wrong OR CONSTANT byte -> srvd val text" while disclaiming the first-byte
    label: M_LASTBYTE showed the claim false for the wide row (all four bytes zero).  Now `srvd half text' serves a
    len-2 read whose two bytes DIFFER (0x56 0x78), so a byte-selection mutant reds; the claim is reworded to the rows.
    The width LABEL is now load-bearing at len 1 (`srvd val text', `wr val text') and len 2 (`srvd half text'), not only
    at len 4.  The len-2 disclosure is CLOSED by the same session (`half wr rdawr': a len-2 write declined); len 8 stays
    disclosed.  M_RELOAD is DISCLOSED as unrowable and listed under the denominator's exclusions, with its reason.
  - GUARD FIELD vs BODY FIELD -- guard: warned_unimpl bit relative_addr.  Body: writeflag, idata, len, data[0].  The
    OFFSET axis is not body-tested (no `if' on relative_addr in the arm; the switch tests cased vs uncased only), so
    per-offset cells are NOT floor -- but pass 3 measured member 6 and the latch SET conditioned on offset CLASS
    passing 36 rows, so one read-back and one count per CLASS are appended (in-word: 0x06; unnamed: 0x21; the named
    class already had ieicr/ieerr rdawr and latch sameoff).  A single-offset scoping beyond those stays disclosed.
  - CHANNEL -- per new row: wr val text / wide wr val: announce TEXT under DKV/DKD/DKT; srvd val text (reworded): TEXT
    under DKZ/DKY; srvd half text: TEXT under DKL/DKY and FATE under DKH; half wr rdawr: VALUE under DKG/DKB/DK2/DKX and
    FATE under DKH; srvd rd latch / srvd wr latch: VALUE under DKX (and DKG/DKB for the write-set one); inword msg /
    unnamed msg: COUNT under DKC (and DKU/DK1/DKE); inword rdawr / unnamed rdawr: VALUE under DKG/DKB/DK2.

PREDICTIONS (46-row instrument; the ten pass-3 mutants ALSO on the 36-row pass-3 instrument, where 36/36 is the
measured breach -- except M_RELOAD, whose 46/46 is the disclosed expectation; MINIMUM red set per mutant):

ACCEPTED, REPAIRED IN-ROUND (L1: the detector is uncommitted; the class closure per the repair-chain amendment)
 1. (opus5, MEASURED) M_WRVAL0 / M_WRDATA0 -- the write announce's value: `wr val text', `wide wr val'.

    "wrong or constant byte" claim: reworded to the rows.
 3. (opus5, MEASURED) M_BACKEDLOW, M_LATCHOFF -- one read-back and one count per offset class (`inword msg/rdawr',
    `unnamed msg/rdawr').  NOT 43 value rows (the #392 shape); single-offset scoping DISCLOSED.

 5. (codex, derived) `len' -> `4' -- `srvd val text' reworded to `0x5 (len 1) -- served 0x34'; `wr val text' carries `(len 1)'.

    pass-2 len-2 disclosure is CLOSED; len 8 stays disclosed.

    end-span path where every reachable start byte is 0); `data[0]' stays the operand, with the end-span reason in the
    comment (fable5) -- Fable's "ticks run between instructions" premise REFUTED by reading timer.c (the SIGALRM handler
    calls the callbacks directly); grok's narrowing (COUNT words only, CEN off at init) adopted; codex's "twelve
    interiors" -> six.

## QUESTIONS

1. **IS THE REPAIR RIGHT?**  Ten rows, two encoders (`LD_HU' 0x02, `ST_H' 0x0a -- check them against `cpu_m88k_instr.c'
   or the width probe), the srvd session's register plan (r4 0x34, r6 0x5678, r8 0x34, r9 0x78; r7 = 0xaabb), the
   alignment of every new access (0x06 halfword, 0x07 byte, 0x21 byte).  Does any new row's text anchor collide with
   another announce in its session (e.g. `0x6 (len 2)' vs a write text)?  Is `srvd wr latch' 0x78 -- i.e. does the
   declined st.b at 0x07 leave the array byte the cased word store put there?
2. **DOES EACH OF THE TWENTY-TWO MUTANTS DIE FOR THE REASON CLAIMED?**  Table 4 and its predictions are inlined; the ten
   pass-3 mutants carry a second score on the pass-3 instrument (36/36 owed = the breach; M_RELOAD 46/46 on the new one
   is disclosed).  A prediction that missed is the interesting case; a mutant killed by a row OTHER than the one built
   for it is a row killing by accident.
3. **THE MIRRORS.**  For each of the ten new rows name its mirror cell (the other arm, the other setter direction, the
   other width, the other offset class) and say whether that cell is rowed, disclosed, or NEITHER.  Neither is the
   finding.
4. **THE SMALLEST EDIT THAT BREAKS THE PROPERTY AND PASSES 46 ROWS.**  Name the row you expect to stay green and why.
   Measured beats derived (Opus built six last pass; derived escapes have been right three times and wrong zero -- but
   they are predictions until measured).
5. **IS THE RECORD HONEST?**  The CHANGELOG block (pass 2, pass 3, table 4, gate sections inlined), the contract
   (docstring, v3), the pass-4 PRETABLE.  Name any sentence a measurement refutes, any count without a deriver, any
   correction that landed only in a later paragraph wXCLUSIONS (M_RELOAD,
   len 8, end-span reads, single-offset scoping, per-instance latch scope, the `debug()'-for-`fatal()' probe-shape
   limit).  If you say close, say what the denominator excludes; if you say not close, name the cell and the edit.

If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.

## THE ARM AS IT NOW STANDS (from `default:` to the handler's `return 1`, VERBATIM)

```c
	default:
		/*  #501 (pcc2defaultkill): this arm ended the host with exit(1) on ANY
		    access to an offset with no case -- 43 of the 0x40 byte offsets:
		    eighteen documented registers the model does not implement (IEICR,
		    IEERR, IEBERR, GPIO, SCSIERR, the printer block, SPEED, the *IACKs),
		    the bytes inside the four counter/compare words, and the unnamed
		    ones -- READS included, though the memcpy above the switch had
		    already served them.  A guest ld.bu or st.b at any of them was a host
		    kill (rung-3 witness).  Now: a READ keeps the served byte (the array's
		    current byte, said so -- inside a running COUNT word that is the
		    tick's value, not a default); a WRITE is DECLINED.  Not a
		    backed store: the cased siblings contradict one (SCCERR clears on
		    write, the ICRs mask and reassert, PSCALECNT/SPEED/the IACKs are
		    read-only), no inspected OpenBSD driver path reaches them (if_ie never
		    attaches: unreadable sits at its 82596 address), and a store from the
		    one arm 43 offsets reach would add a bounded-write surface.  One
		    latched fatal() per offset per instance, the house `(once)' shape --
		    unlike PSCALEADJ's UNLATCHED print above (pcc2pscaleflood).  The
		    IACK offsets 0x23/0x25 are declined like the rest: modelling them (on
		    the SCCRXIACK shape) would open two new entries into the C040 path,
		    so pcc2c040kill goes first.  The %= fold above IS the real part's:
		    Motorola VMESBCP2 Table 6-1 lists $FFF42040-$FFF42FFF as repeated
		    PCCchip2 registers.  */
		if (!(d->warned_unimpl & (1ULL << relative_addr))) {
			d->warned_unimpl |= 1ULL << relative_addr;
			if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
				fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented write to offset "
				    "0x%x (len %d): 0x%x -- declined.  (once) ]\n",
				    (int) relative_addr, (int) len, (int) idata);
			else
				/*  data[0] is the byte actually SERVED; a reload of the
				    array could differ: on the end-span path (zero-filled,
				    the array byte kept) and inside a running COUNT word,
				    whose SIGALRM-driven tick (timer.c) can land between
				    the copy and this line.  A wide read is labelled.  */
				fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented read from offset "
				    "0x%x (len %d) -- served 0x%02x.  (once) ]\n",
				    (int) relative_addr, (int) len, data[0]);
		}
		break;
	}

	return 1;
```

## THE DETECTOR (`regress/pcc2_default_probe.py`, VERBATIM, contract included)

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""pcc2defaultkill DETECTOR (#501) -- the `default:' arm of DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2) in dev_pcc2.c.

Before #501 that arm ended the emulator process (`exit(1)') on ANY access to an offset with no `case'
-- READS included, although the memcpy above the switch had already served them.  Forty-three of the
0x40 byte offsets reach it (derived from mvme_pcctworeg.h against the case list): eighteen NAMED
registers the model does not implement (PSCALECNT, GPIO_ICR/PCR, SCCMOIACK, SCCTXIACK, IEERR, IEICR,
IEBERR, SCSIERR, the printer block, SPEED, PRTDATA), the bytes INSIDE the four 32-bit counter/compare
words, and the unnamed offsets.  A single guest `st.b' or `ld.bu' at any of them was a host kill
(rung-3 witness, 7/13 on the committed build: regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/witness_20260905/).

THE FIX (pass-1 panel of 2026-09-05, nine seats, converged): READ side -- keep the byte the memcpy
already served (the register array's CURRENT byte -- the tick's, inside a running COUNT word: the announce says so);
WRITE side -- DECLINE + a per-offset latched `fatal()' ("(once)", the house idiom), NOT a backed
accept: the file's own cased siblings contradict a blanket store (SCCERR is write-to-clear, the ICRs
mask and reassert, PSCALECNT/SPEED/the IACKs are read-only), the addition licence is unmet (no guest
reaches these offsets on this model's inspected driver paths -- if_ie never attaches), and a store into pcctwo_reg[] from the
one arm reached by 43 offsets adds a bounded-write surface the decline does not have.

CONTRACT -- what this detector is built to catch, by row (46 rows: 23 at pass 2, +13 by the pass-2 repair, +10 by the
pass-3 repair; every pass-3 rurn enc(0x0b, rs, 2, off)
def ST_W(rs, off): return enc(0x09, rs, 2, off)
def LD_HU(rd, off): return enc(0x02, rd, 2, off)   # pass 3: a len-2 default read
def ST_H(rs, off): return enc(0x0a, rs, 2, off)    # pass 3: a len-2 default write
I_ST_SCR = enc(0x09, 5, 3, 0); I_LD_SCR = enc(0x05, 4, 3, 0); I_BR_SELF = 0xc0000000
def program(insns, at=CODE):
    words = list(insns) + [I_BR_SELF, 0]
    return ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * k, w) for k, w in enumerate(words)] + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(insns)]
def setup(r5="0x5a5a5a5a"):
    return ["r2=0x%x" % PCC2, "r3=0x%x" % SCRATCH, "r5=%s" % r5, "r4=0x%x" % SENTINEL]
def printed(t, reg="r4"):
    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\r?\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
    return "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16) if m else "absent"
def fate(alive, how): return "survived" if alive else "host " + how
def val(t, alive, how): return printed(t) if alive else fate(alive, how)
rows, bad = [], []
def row(name, got, want, why):
    ok = str(got) == str(want)
    rows.append(nathe gate, L1).
    print("%-16s %-14s %-14s %-60s %s" % (name, got, want, why[:60], "ok" if ok else "FAIL"), flush=True)
print("pcc2defaultkill DETECTOR -- binary=%s label=%s" % (BIN, LABEL))
print("%-16s %-14s %-14s %-60s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "notved", "guest ld.bu at all 18 in one session: the READ side of the defect; a writeflag guard that re-kills reads dies here")
row("named rd msg", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 18, "the read announce fires once per offset too")
row("named rd srvd", t.counork header does not name (the manual names some: 0x39 is SPEED's 2nd byte, 0x3b PRTDATA's)")
row("unnamed msg", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 13, "...thirteen announces; the second write to 0x21 and its read-back are silent (per-offset latch, unnamed class)")
row("unnamed rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "...and 0x21 reads back 0x0: declined in the unnamed class too (a member 6 scoped by offset class reads 0x5a)")
t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([LD_BU(4, o) for o in UNNAMED]))
row("unnamed rd", fate(alive, how), "survived", "...and a guest ld.bu at each of the thirteen: with named rd + inword rd every one of the 43 is read once")
# -- the disposition-pinning rows: read-after-write in ONE session
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), LD_BU(4, IEICR)]) + ["print r4"])
row("ieicr rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "st.b 0x5a then ld.bu at IEICR in ONE session reads 0x0: the write was DECLINED (a backed accept reads 0x5a)")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEERR), LD_BU(4, IEERR)]) + ["print r4"])
row("ieerr rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "the same at IEERR (0x28), the write-to-clear class whose cased sibling SCCERR clears on write")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, SCCERR), LD_BU(4, SCCERR)]) + ["print r4"])
row("sccerr rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "CONTROL: the CASED write-to-clear sibling reads 0x0 under either disposition -- makes the two rows above attributable")
# -- the latch: per offset, per instance
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), ST_B(5, IEICR), LD_BU(4, IEICR)]) + ["print r4"])
row("latch sameoff", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 1, "two writes to one offset announce ONCE: an unlatched arm prints 2 (the read-back is silent: bit set)")
row("sameoff val", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "...and the SECOND write (latch already set) is declined too: a store exempting the first write reads 0x5a (Opus M_O2, MEASURED 23/23 escape)")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), ST_B(5, IEBERR)]))
row("latch twooff", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 2, "two offsets announce TWICE: a single whole-arm latch bit prints 1")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), LD_BU(4, IEICRST_B(5, IEICR)]))
row("latch aba", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 2, "A,B,A announces TWICE: `|=' -> `=' forgets A when B announces and prints 3 (codex) -- earlier bits survive a new offset")
# -- the fold and the text
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5=", 1, "...and the announce names the FOLDED offset 0x2a: a changed fold names another")
t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, PRTICR)]))
row("dir wr text", t.count("unimplemented write to") if alive else "n/a", 1, "the announce says WR swallowed no cased arm")
t, alive, how = session(["put b 0xfff4202a, 0x00"])
row("put b ctl", fate(alive, how), "survived", "CONTROL: the debugger's put b never reaches a DM_DEFAULT arm (measured before the fix too)")
row("put b ctl msg", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 0, "...so it prints nothing -- not a witness, a control")
print("PCC2D_RESULT=%d/%d" % (len(rows) - len(bad), len(rows)))
if bad:
    print("PCC2D_RED=%s" % ",".join(bad))
print("PCC2D_PROBE_DONE")

```

## THE CHANGELOG BLOCK (as it stands)

```
# Round #501 pcc2defaultkill -- the `default:` arm of DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2) ended the host on any access to an uncased offset, reads included; now reads keep the served byte and writes are declined, one latched announce per offset

**2026-09-05.  HIGH, ranked first by the 2026-09-04 fix-review adjudication.  Product change: `src/devicerived from `mvme_pcctworeg.h` against the 21 labels; two seats
re-derived it): eighteen NAMED registers the model does not implement (PSCALECNT, GPIO_ICR/PCR, SCCMOIACK, SCCTXIACK,
IEERR, IEICR, IEBERR, SCSIERR, the printer block, SPEED, PRTDATA), the twed.bu` there likewise -- the READ side kills too; IEBERR and the
in-word byte 0x05 and the alias 0x6a kill the same way.  Liveness (the probe's scratch round-trip) and
device-signature (CHIPID reads PCC2_ID 0x20) controls pass.  **The debugger's `put b` does NOT reach the arm** -- it
survives and prints nothing -- which the filing had called the cheapest witness (measured false; corrected).  The
witness script is the width probe's own `session()` helper: a first draft hand-rolled the pty wait, accepted a
STALE prompt, and read every kill as "survived" -- the carrier's rule about hand-rolled pty plumbing, re-learned.
The combined IEBERR/IEERR row measures IEBERR only (the exiting build dies at the first); IEERR's kill follows from
source (codex).  **13/13 on the fixed build.**

## The design (pass 1: nine seats, `_scratchpad/panel_20260905_021751`, converged)

READ: keep the byte the memcpy served and SAY it is served -- it is the register array's CURRENT byte (inside a running
COUNT word, the tick's; the pass-ants the seats measured or read:
the file's own cased siblings contradict a blanket store (SCCERR is write-to-clear -- the board manual's SCLR bits
read as zero; the ICRs mask and reassert; PSCALECNT/SPEED/the IACKs are read-only); the addition licence is unmet
(no inspected OpenBSD driver path reaches these offsets on this model -- the rung-3 witness is itself a guest, so the
pass-1 wording "no guest reaches" was too wide: codex, pass 2); a store from the one arm 43 offsets reach adds a bounded-write
surface into `pcctwo_reg[]` at 0x3d.  The Opus seat BUILT both candidates in a scratch clone and measured the
13-row witness at 13/13 under BOTH -- disposition-blind -- so the detector carries read-after-write rows in ONE
session (`ieicr rdawr`, `ieerr rdawr`: store 0x5a then load, want 0x0 -- a backed accept reads 0x5a) with the CASED
write-to-clear sibling `sccerr rdawr` as the control that reads 0x0 under either.

**Two premises of the pass-1 brief were wrong and are corrected here.**  The in-tree guest driver (`if_ie.c`) writes
three of these offsets, but NEVER RUNS on this model: `machine_mvme88k.c:106` installs `unreadable` at the 82596
address, `if_ie.c:407-409` fails its match on `badpaddr()` there, and `ie_setupram()` returns early at `:561-564`
before the PCC2 store at `:609` (Fable, Opus, grok, codex independently; verified).  `ie0` IS configured in
`conf/M187` and `conf/NFS` -- the brief's conf grep looked in the wrong files.  So the HIGH ranking rests on the
witness and severity, not on the driver.  And the alias the model implements (`relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE`) is the
REAL part's: Motorola VMESBCP2 Table 6-1 lists `$FFF42040-$FFF42FFF  PCCchip2 Registers  Repeated` -- the codex seat
cited it, the manual was fetched into `_scratchpad/` and the line read (`vmesbcp2.txt:4629`).  Two seats had rightly
called the fold unestablished before the manual was on disk.

Recorded alternatives: the codex seat's ONE latch for the whole site, writes only (not adopted: a driver scanning
registers should see each distword bytes are the interior of IMPLEMENTED counters, declined
here as a width/alignment matter (`pcc2widecompose`), not claimed as unimplemented registers; the IACK offsets
0x23/0x25 are declined like the rest -- modelling them on the SCCRXIACK shape would open two new entries into the
C040 path, so `pcc2c040kill` goes first (filed as a cross-round obligation); per-instance latch scope is not
provable by two fresh sessions (each is a new process) -- a two-instance fixture is filed.

## The detector as it shipped at PASS 2 (`regress/pcc2_default_probe.py`, 23 rows then; 46 now -- see the pass-2 and pass-3 sections; gate 14 pins all 46)

Controls: `live`, `devsig chipid`, `sccerr rdawr` (cased sibling), `genctl rt`, `t1cmp word rt` (the cased arms are
untouchei, pass 2) -- an arithmetic slip in the
pre-table, not a channel surprise; M_RDKILL's read-after-write rows contain a LOAD, so they die with the reads.  Every
mutant string was gcc-syntax-checked before the table (`mut_syntax2.py`, anchors from the driver'nd gate 14 -- which counts rows by `^<name>  .*ok$` -- then counted
ZERO of 23 rows while the probe said DONE (`killtable_20260905/gate14_501_FAIL_rowformat.log`, 25 of 844 checks
red, the 251 width rows all green).  L1, fixed in-round: status last, as the width probe prints; the gate's own
patterns then count 23.  Run 2 (05:05-05:18, frozen `a96b8bcf`) re-measured the same six mutants on the fixed-format
instrument -- BASE 23, M_EXIT 7, M_UNLATCH 21, M_ONELATCH 19, M_BACKED 21, M_RDKILL 16, M_DIRSWAP 20 -- identical
to run 1 row for row; run 1 is kept as `run1_oldrowformat/`.  *A probe whose summary line is right and whose rows
the gate cannot count is a detector whose OWN summary reads green while the gate counts nothing of it -- and the
gate is the check that saw it, FAIL 25/844 (the first draft said "would have shipped green", which overstated: it
could not have shipped past that gate -- codex, pass 2), which is what a gate is for.*

## Gate 14 (`killtable_20260905/gate14_501.log`; the live probes -- default md5 a96b8bcf, width a080e2bc, both equal to the frozen run-2 pair; run alone 05:19-05:55)

## Pass 2 (nine seats on the real diff, and was RE-FIRED ONCE with the 19 KB packet inline; it then
answered), glm and deepseek (thinking-burned on the full packet -- `ollama_*.burn.txt` -- and answered 2 KB
one-question mini packets), minimax, Opus 5 and Fable 5 (Agent seats; answers preserved hift (`uint64_t`, `%= 0x40`), `break` reaches the one `return 1`, the latch words are separate

old code called `exit(1)`, so no boot that would not see it |
|---|---|---|
| Opus (MEASURED 23/23) | the read announce's value `-> 0` (one token) | `named rd srvd` counts the PHRASE `served 0x`, never the value; no row served a nonzero byte |
| Opus (MEASURED 23/23) | member 6 on the LATCHED path: tioned on writes | the bit-set cell was reached only through a WRITE (`rw one`); two reads at one offset had no row -- the setter's direction is a third axis the (writeflag x latch) 2x2 hides |

**The diagnosis, from the pass-1 PRETABLE's own GUARD-FIELD line:** *"they disagree only in the latch's own state,
which the sameoff/twooff/rw-one rows build"* -- true, and the state was observed on one channel (the announce count)
while the mutant that matters (Opus M_O2) changes the OTHER channel (the read-back value) in exactly that state.  The
guard/body check found the cell and the row was written against thes the buffer before device dispatch).
`data[0]` is definitionally what was served -- the right operand under every reading.

**The repair (one diff, class closure -- the repair-chain amendment: consecutive cells of one class are closed
together, not one per pass).**  PRODUCT: both announces carry `(len %d)`; the read announce prints `data[0]` -- the
byte actually SERVED -- with a comment saying why.  DETECTOR, 36 rows (+13): `latch rd rd` and `latch rd wr` complete
the setter x tester product (W,W `sameoff`; W,R `rw one`; R,R; R,W); `latch aba` pins accumulation across offsets;
`sameoff val` reads back after the SECOND write (the latched path's disposition); `srvd val` + `srvd val text` serve
a byte the GUEST wrote (a word store through the cased T1CMP arm lve mutant shapes, named above; the cells they close are the setter x tester direction
product (four), accumulation across offsets, the disposition on the latched path, the served VALUE, wide (len 4)
default accesses in both directions, and every one of the 43 uncased offsets driven once in each direction.  NOT
closed, disclosed in the contract: len-2-only re-kills; an end-spanning wide read; per-instance latch scope.

**Two runs, one prediction file.**  Run 1 (06:26-06:35) measured BASE (36/36, 23/23) and M_WRLATCH (34/36, 23/23) --
identical to run 2 -- and then DIED at its second `mut` line: an apostrophe inside a single-quoted expect string
(`len != 1'`) ended the quote and bash aborted the script with a parse error, while the wrapper reported rc=0 (its
trailing echo).  `mut_syntax2.py` had checked all twelve mutants' C and could not see a shell-quoting defect in the
driver; `bash -n` had been run on the gate script and not on the driver.  The partial run is kept as
`run1_partial_quoting/`; the generator now refuses a quote in an expect string and the launcher runs `bash -n` on
the frozen driver before exec.  Run 2 (06:37-07:26) is the table above.  The clone's source was `cmp`-clean against
SEC and its sentinel cleared after the restore rebuild.

## Pass 3 (nine seats on the pass-2 repair diff, `_scratchpad/panel_20260905_081718`): the repair had reproduced the breach it closed, on the other arm

**Roster.**  codex (GPT-6 Astra, max; a reconnect storm mid-run, then a 9 KB final answer), grok, kimi (the 81 KB pointer

Fable GO-WITH-CHANGES after two L4s, codex NO-GO, Opus NOT CLOSE -- **and Opus MEASURED.**

**Six clean sweeps of the 36-row instrument, measured by the Opus seat in its own scratch clone** (BASE reproduced at
36/36; instrument md5 verified equal to the frozen table-3 pair; every mutant two diff lines, rebuilt from a removed
`.o`, probed with the unedited instrument; sentinel discipline and a `RESTORE_CMP_CLEAN_VS_SEC` line; nothing written to
any project tree):

| mutant | edit | why 36 rows could not see it |
|---|---|---|
| M_WRVAL0 | the WRITE announce's `(int) idata` -> `0` | **the mirror of pass 2's M_O1**: the value channel was pinned on the READ arm only (`srvd val text`); every write-side row counts a phrase -- `#499` reproduced inside its own repair |
| M_WRDATA0 | `(int) idata` -> `data[0]` | same cell; the edit the new symmetric-looking comment invites -- prints `0x5a` for a `0x5a5a5a5a` word write |
| M_LASTBYTE | the READ announce's `data[0]` -> `data[len-1]` | `wide rd srvd` reads PRTICR, whose four bytes are all zero and unreachable by a guest write -- its channel CANNOT VARY under a byte-selection mutant: the pre-table CHANNEL check's own failure mode, and the contract had claimed "a wrong or constant byte -> `srvd val text`" |
| M_RELOAD | `data[0]` -> `d->pcctwo_reg[relative_addr]` (reverts the pass-2 product repair) | the two differ only in a race (the COUNT words, while a counter runs, under the SIGALRM-driven tick) or on the end-span path, where every reachable start offset's array byte is 0 -- **no deterministic row can pin it**: DISCLOSED |
| M_BACKEDLOW | `if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && relative_addr < 0x14) pcctwo_reg[ra] = idata;` | member 6 conditioned off the two offsets that read back: the FATE axis was closed at 43 offsets, the DISPOSITION axis at two (kimi derived the same store, M_STORE1) |
| M_LATCHOFF | the latch SET restricted to IEICR/IEERR | the same class on the latch axis: every count row lives at the named offsets |

codex, reading, DERIVED three more that pass 36 rows: **M_LATCH_RD_ZERO** -- `else if (writeflag == MEM_READ) data[0] =
0;` on the latched path, the mirror of M_O2 on the READ response channel (every rowed read after a preceding access
expected 0 anyway; `srvd val` read its nonzero byte with the latch clear); and the width label made constant on either
arm (`(int) len` -> `4`: no byte row required `(len 1)`).  grok and Fable named `if (len == 2) exit(1);` -- the len-2
re-kill the pass-2 contract had DISCLOSED.  kimi named the write-value hole independently (M_WVAL0).

**Adjudication (count the arguments).**  The three close verdicts and Fable's had not built the edits; Opus had.  The
write-announce value is the cell the pass-2 repair should have closed with its mirror: two consecutive passes found the
value channel unpinned, one arm each -- the repair-chain amendment's trigger -- so the class is closed in one diff here:
**ten rows, every one APPENDED to an existing session** (zero new sessions): `wr val text` and `wide wr val` (the write
value at len 1 and len 4; the wide one separates `idata` from `data[0]`); `srvd half text` and `half wr rdawr` (a len-2
read served and announced from its FIRST byte, 0x56 of 0x5678 -- two bytes that differ, so a byte-selection mutant
reds -- and a len-2 write declined: the len-2 disclosure CLOSED); `srvd rd latch` and `srvd wr latch` (a nonzero byte
re-read after its bit was set by a read and by a write); `inword msg`/`inword rdawr` and `unnamed msg`/`unnamed rdawr`
(one announce count and one read-back per remaining offset CLASS, the named class already having `latch sameoff` and
the `rdawr` rows); and `srvd val text` reworded to count `0x5 (len 1) -- served 0x34` so the width label is
load-bearing at len 1.  Two encoders (`LD_HU`, `ST_H`) borrowed from the width probe.  The product is untouched except
two comment sentences.  46 rows; every name still under 14 characters.

**Dispositions the pass changed or narrowed.**  F2 #7 (codex's tick reason for `data[0]`): grok narrowed it -- the tick
writes back only T1COUNT/T2COUNT and only while a counter runs, so under this instrument `data[0]` and a reload are the
same byte and `srvd val text` pins the constant-0 mutant, not the reload; Fable REFUTED it -- "ticks run between guest
instructions, not inside `memory_rw`" -- and that refutation is itself refuted by reading `src/core/timer.c`:
`timer_tick(int signal_nr)` is the SIGALRM handler and calls `timer->timer_tick(timer, timer->extra)` directly, while
`dev_pcc2.c` registers `pcc_timer_tick` with `timer_add`, so the pcc2 tick is signal-driven and can land in the
copy-to-announce gap.  Fable's second reason needs no timing and is added to the comment: on the `#119` end-span path
the read is zero-filled while the array byte is kept.  Opus adds a third backstop, `memory_rw.c` `#95` pre-zeroing the
buffer before dispatch.  `data[0]` -- what was served -- is the right operand under every reading; M_RELOAD is disclosed
as unrowable, with that reason.  The kimi/grok/Opus offset-class residual is closed per CLASS and disclosed per single
offset (`#392` if rowed per offset); the write-announce value is closed; len 8 (`ld.d`/`st.d` exist in the m88k core)
stays disclosed beside end-span reads and per-instance latch scope; the "F2 #10 safety sentence" is narrowed (codex): it
says only that no previously successful trace entered the arm, not that the surviving arm is boot-validated.  Rejected:
minimax's and deepseek's close verdicts were readings of the packet and are overtaken by the measurements.  The pass-2
`pcc2dispall` filing is WITHDRAWN as filed -- its two items are now rows.

**Records defects found by the pass, corrected in place** (codex, grok, Opus, Fable): the gate paragraph's "875 checks"
-- the gate's own line says `PASS (857 checks)` and `grep -c ' ok '` had counted 18 nested probe lines; the opening
summary and the detector heading still said 23 rows (now 46, with the history); the four pass-1 sentences the pass-2
section had corrected only in a LATER paragraph -- "the array's default", "no guest reaches", "listed fifteen", "would
have shipped green" -- are now corrected in their own sentences with the first wording quoted; the table-3 section did
not record the `latch sameoff` value miss (predicted 2 under M_UNLATCH, measured 3 -- the appended read-back announces
too; red either way); the "twelve interiors" tick rationale (six); the pass-3 `SEATS.txt` says kimi MISSING because it
was written before the kimi answer landed (the file stands; this sentence is the reconciliation).

## Table 4 (`killtable4_20260905/driver.log`; predictions in `PRETABLE_501c.out`, mtime 09:08:39, table launched 09:09; frozen repaired instrument md5 `b9b2fadc` == live; the pass-3 instrument `old36/` md5 `10737375`; scratch clone of build/, BASE rebuilt in the clone, twenty-two mutants each compile-checked before the table and proven by a diff count; driver parse-checked before exec)

**Every one of the ten pass-3 escapes is MEASURED on the same binary by both instruments: 36/36 on the instrument that
shipped at pass 3, red on the repaired one on exactly the rows built for it -- and the disclosed exception, M_RELOAD,
measured 46/46 on the repaired instrument as the contract predicts.**  Seventeen of twenty-two predictions were EXACT in
score and red set; five missed in the SAFE direction only (extra reds, no predicted red went green), all of one class,
named below with their values.

| mutant | shape | repaired instrument (46 rows) | red rows | pass-3 instrument (36) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BASE | the repaired working set | **46/46** | -- | **36/36** (control) |
| M_WRVAL0 | write announce value `-> 0` (Opus) | 44/46 | wr val text, wide wr val | **36/36** -- the breach |
| M_WRDATA0 | write announce value `-> data[0]` (Opus) | 45/46 | wide wr val (a byte write's idata IS data[0], so `wr val text` stays -- as predicted) | **36/36** |
| M_LASTBYTE | read announce `data[len-1]` (Opus) | 45/46 | srvd half text (says `served 0x78`) | **36/36** |
| M_RELOAD | read announce reloads the array (Opus) | **46/46 -- DISCLOSED ESCAPE, as predicted** | none: no deterministic row can pin it (a race in the COUNT words under the signal-driven tick, or the end-span path where every reachable start byte is 0) | **36/36** |
| M_BACKEDLOW | member 6 for `relative_addr < 0x14` (Opus) | 43/46 | inword rdawr (0x5a), half wr rdawr (0xbb78), srvd wr latch (0xbb) | **36/36** |
| M_LATCHOFF | latch SET only at IEICR/IEERR (Opus) | 42/46 (pred 43) | inword msg 14, unnamed msg 15, srvd half text 2, **+ srvd val text 2** (safe miss, see below) | **36/36** |
| M_HALFKILL | `if (len == 2) exit(1);` (grok, Fable) | 40/46 | the six srvd-session rows (the session dies at its `ld.hu`) | **36/36** (no len-2 access there) |
| M_LATCHRD0 | `else if (writeflag == MEM_READ) data[0] = 0;` (codex, derived) | 43/46 | srvd rd latch (0x0), srvd wr latch (0x0), half wr rdawr (0x78) | **36/36** |
| M_LEN4R | read announce `(int) len -> 4` (codex, derived) | 44/46 | srvd val text, srvd half text | **36/36** |
| M_LEN4W | write announce `(int) len -> 4` (codex, derived) | 45/46 | wr val text | **36/36** |
| M_EXIT | (b) the verbatim `exit(1)` arm | 7/46 | the 29 of table 3 + all ten new rows | (table 3: 7/36) |
| M_UNLATCH | (d) announce unlatched | 36/46 (pred 37) | the six of table 3 (latch sameoff 3) + inword msg 14, unnamed msg 15, srvd half text 2, **+ srvd val text 2** | (30/36) |
| M_ONELATCH | (d) one whole-arm bit | 38/46 | the five of table 3 + inword msg 1, unnamed msg 1, srvd half text 0 | (31/36) |
| M_BACKED | member 6, every write | 38/46 | the four of table 3 + inword rdawr, unnamed rdawr (0x5a), half wr rdawr (0xaabb), srvd wr latch (0xbb) | (32/36) |
| M_RDKILL | (d) reads re-killed | 16/46 (pred 18) | the 19 of table 3 + the nine predicted + **inword wr, unnamed wr** (their sessions now END in a read the mutant kills) | (17/36) |
| M_DIRSWAP | (d) direction test inverted | 37/46 | the six of table 3 + wr val text, wide wr val, srvd half text | (30/36) |
| M_WRLATCH | latch SET only on writes (grok A) | 43/46 (pred 44) | latch rd rd, latch rd wr, **+ srvd val text 2** | (34/36) |
| M_WIDEKILL | `if (len != 1) exit(1);` (grok B) | 35/46 | the four of table 3 + wide wr val + the six srvd-session rows | (32/36) |
| M_OFFKILL | `if (relative_addr == 0x06) exit(1);` (Fable) | 36/46 | the two of table 3 + inword msg, inword rdawr + the six srvd-session rows | (34/36) |
| M_SRVD0 | read announce value `-> 0` (Opus, pass 2) | 44/46 | srvd val text, srvd half text | (35/36) |
| M_BACKED2 | member 6 on the latched path (Opus, pass 2) | 42/46 | sameoff val, inword rdawr, unnamed rdawr (0x5a), half wr rdawr (0xbb78) -- `srvd wr latch` stays 0x78: the st.b at 0x07 is a FIRST write there, declined | (35/36) |
| M_ABA | `\|=` `->` `=` (codex, pass 2) | 42/46 (pred 43) | latch aba 3, inword msg 13, unnamed msg 14, **+ srvd val text 2** | (35/36) |

**The five safe misses are ONE class, recorded with their values:** the repair appended a re-read of 0x05 to the served-value
session (`LD_BU(8, 0x05)`, the `srvd rd latch` row), so under any mutant that leaves 0x05 UNLATCHED at that point -- the
latch disabled (M_UNLATCH), scoped to IEICR/IEERR (M_LATCHOFF), set only on writes (M_WRLATCH), or forgotten by `=`
(M_ABA) -- the re-read announces a second `0x5 (len 1) -- served 0x34` and `srvd val text` counts 2 against a want of 1.
The prediction listed `srvd half text 2` for the same reason and missed its neighbour.  M_RDKILL's two extra reds are the
in-word and unnamed WRITE rows, whose sessions now end in the read-back the mutant kills.  Every predicted red went red;
no predicted red stayed green.  The table-3 section's late-recorded `latch sameoff 3` was the same family.

Denominator of this zero: twenty-two mutant shapes, named above; the cells closed are the announce's five channels (fate,
count, direction, width, value) on BOTH arms; the setter x tester direction product (four); accumulation across offsets;
the disposition AND the read response on the latched path; served value at len 1 and len 2; widths 1, 2 and 4 in both
directions; every one of the 43 uncased offsets driven once in each direction, with one announce count and one
read-back per offset CLASS.  EXCLUDED, disclosed in the contract: the reload of the array (M_RELOAD, measured 46/46 --
outside the contract by construction); len 8; an end-spanning wide read; a member 6 or a latch scoped to a SINGLE
unsampled offset; per-instance latch scope; `debug()` for `fatal()` (the probe steps, so both print).

One run.  The clone's source was `cmp`-clean against SEC after the restore rebuild and its sentinel cleared.

## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

 189: ## QUESTIONS
 190: 
 191: 1. **IS THE REPAIR RIGHT?**  Ten rows, two encoders (`LD_HU' 0x02, `ST_H' 0x0a -- check them against `cpu_m88k_instr.c'
 192:    or the width probe), the srvd session's register plan (r4 0x34, r6 0x5678, r8 0x34, r9 0x78; r7 = 0xaabb), the
 193:    alignment of every new access (0x06 halfword, 0x07 byte, 0x21 byte).  Does any new row's text anchor collide with
 194:    another announce in its session (e.g. `0x6 (len 2)' vs a write text)?  Is `srvd wr latch' 0x78 -- i.e. does the
 195:    declined st.b at 0x07 leave the array byte the cased word store put there?
 196: 2. **DOES EACH OF THE TWENTY-TWO MUTANTS DIE FOR THE REASON CLAIMED?**  Table 4 and its predictions are inlined; the ten
 197:    pass-3 mutants carry a second score on the pass-3 instrument (36/36 owed = the breach; M_RELOAD 46/46 on the new one
 198:    is disclosed).  A prediction that missed is the interesting case; a mutant killed by a row OTHER than the one built
 199:    for it is a row killing by accident.
 200: 3. **THE MIRRORS.**  For each of the ten new rows name its mirror cell (the other arm, the other setter direction, the
 201:    other width, the other offset class) and say whether that cell is rowed, disclosed, or NEITHER.  Neither is the
 202:    finding.
 203: 4. **THE SMALLEST EDIT THAT BREAKS THE PROPERTY AND PASSES 46 ROWS.**  Name the row you expect to stay green and why.
 204:    Measured beats derived (Opus built six last pass; derived escapes have been right three times and wrong zero -- but
 205:    they are predictions until measured).
 206: 5. **IS THE RECORD HONEST?**  The CHANGELOG block (pass 2, pass 3, table 4, gate sections inlined), the contract
 207:    (docstring, v3), the pass-4 PRETABLE.  Name any sentence a measurement refutes, any count without a deriver, any
 208:    correction that landed only in a later paragraph while the original sentence stands.
 209: 6. **CLOSE?**  A closing zero must be VALID: the diff delivered (inlined), the roster complete or misses named, the zero
 210:    carried WITH its denominator -- which mutant shapes (twenty-two, named), which cells -- and its EXCLUSIONS (M_RELOAD,
 211:    len 8, end-span reads, single-offset scoping, per-instance latch scope, the `debug()'-for-`fatal()' probe-shape
 212:    limit).  If you say close, say what the denominator excludes; if you say not close, name the cell and the edit.
 213: 
 214: If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.

 251: 	default:
 252: 		/*  #501 (pcc2defaultkill): this arm ended the host with exit(1) on ANY
 253: 		    access to an offset with no case -- 43 of the 0x40 byte offsets:

 260: 		    current byte, said so -- inside a running COUNT word that is the

 274: 		if (!(d->warned_unimpl & (1ULL << relative_addr))) {
 275: 			d->warned_unimpl |= 1ULL << relative_addr;

 277: 				fatal("[ pcc2: unimplemented write to offset "

 548: rightly called it unestablished before the manual was on disk); the fork's PCC2_SIZE (0x40) and the guest header's

 580: def enc(op26, rd, rs1, imm): return (op26 << 26) | (rd << 21) | (rs1 << 16) | (imm & 0xffff)
 581: def LD_BU(rd, off): return enc(0x03, rd, 2, off)
 582: def LD_W(rd, off): return enc(0x05, rd, 2, off)
 583: def ST_B(rs, off): return enc(0x0b, rs, 2, off)
 584: def ST_W(rs, off): return enc(0x09, rs, 2, off)
 585: def LD_HU(rd, off): return enc(0x02, rd, 2, off)   # pass 3: a len-2 default read
 586: def ST_H(rs, off): return enc(0x0a, rs, 2, off)    # pass 3: a len-2 default write
 587: I_ST_SCR = enc(0x09, 5, 3, 0); I_LD_SCR = enc(0x05, 4, 3, 0); I_BR_SELF = 0xc0000000
 588: def program(insns, at=CODE):
 589:     words = list(insns) + [I_BR_SELF, 0]
 590:     return ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * k, w) for k, w in enumerate(words)] + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(insns)]
 591: def setup(r5="0x5a5a5a5a"):
 592:     return ["r2=0x%x" % PCC2, "r3=0x%x" % SCRATCH, "r5=%s" % r5, "r4=0x%x" % SENTINEL]
 593: def printed(t, reg="r4"):
 594:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\r?\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 595:     return "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16) if m else "absent"
 596: def fate(alive, how): return "survived" if alive else "host " + how
 597: def val(t, alive, how): return printed(t) if alive else fate(alive, how)
 598: rows, bad = [], []
 599: def row(name, got, want, why):
 600:     ok = str(got) == str(want)
 601:     rows.append(name); (None if ok else bad.append(name))
 602:     #  STATUS LAST: gate 14 counts a row with `^<name>  .*ok$' / ` ok$' / ` FAIL$' -- a first draft printed the

 618: row("named rd msg", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 18, "the read announce fires once per offset too")
 619: row("named rd srvd", t.count("served 0x") if alive else "n/a", 18, "the read announce says what happened -- SERVED, never 'ignored' (the VALUE is pinned by `srvd val text')")
 620: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, o) for o in INWORD] + [ST_B(5, 0x06), LD_BU(4, 0x06)]) + ["print r4"])
 621: row("inword wr", fate(alive, how), "survived", "ALL twelve bytes inside the four 32-bit counter/compare words: no case -> default: (a re-kill at any one dies)")
 622: row("inword msg", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 12, "...twelve announces for twelve offsets; the SECOND write to 0x06 and the read-back are silent: the latch is per offset in THIS class too (Opus M_LATCHOFF)")
 623: row("inword rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "...and 0x06 reads back 0x0 after two writes: DECLINED in this class too -- a member 6 scoped to the low offsets reads 0x5a (Opus M_BACKEDLOW)")
 624: t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([LD_BU(4, o) for o in INWORD]))
 625: row("inword rd", fate(alive, how), "survived", "...and a guest ld.bu at each of the twelve (reads drove 18/43 offsets before this row -- codex, Fable)")
 626: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, o) for o in UNNAMED] + [ST_B(5, 0x21), LD_BU(4, 0x21)]) + ["print r4"])
 627: row("unnamed wr", fate(alive, how), "survived", "ALL thirteen offsets the fork header does not name (the manual names some: 0x39 is SPEED's 2nd byte, 0x3b PRTDATA's)")
 628: row("unnamed msg", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 13, "...thirteen announces; the second write to 0x21 and its read-back are silent (per-offset latch, unnamed class)")
 629: row("unnamed rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "...and 0x21 reads back 0x0: declined in the unnamed class too (a member 6 scoped by offset class reads 0x5a)")
 630: t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([LD_BU(4, o) for o in UNNAMED]))
 631: row("unnamed rd", fate(alive, how), "survived", "...and a guest ld.bu at each of the thirteen: with named rd + inword rd every one of the 43 is read once")
 632: # -- the disposition-pinning rows: read-after-write in ONE session
 633: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), LD_BU(4, IEICR)]) + ["print r4"])
 634: row("ieicr rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "st.b 0x5a then ld.bu at IEICR in ONE session reads 0x0: the write was DECLINED (a backed accept reads 0x5a)")
 635: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEERR), LD_BU(4, IEERR)]) + ["print r4"])
 636: row("ieerr rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "the same at IEERR (0x28), the write-to-clear class whose cased sibling SCCERR clears on write")
 637: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, SCCERR), LD_BU(4, SCCERR)]) + ["print r4"])
 638: row("sccerr rdawr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "CONTROL: the CASED write-to-clear sibling reads 0x0 under either disposition -- makes the two rows above attributable")
 639: # -- the latch: per offset, per instance
 640: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), ST_B(5, IEICR), LD_BU(4, IEICR)]) + ["print r4"])
 641: row("latch sameoff", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 1, "two writes to one offset announce ONCE: an unlatched arm prints 2 (the read-back is silent: bit set)")
 642: row("sameoff val", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "...and the SECOND write (latch already set) is declined too: a store exempting the first write reads 0x5a (Opus M_O2, MEASURED 23/23 escape)")
 643: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), ST_B(5, IEBERR)]))
 644: row("latch twooff", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 2, "two offsets announce TWICE: a single whole-arm latch bit prints 1")
 645: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), LD_BU(4, IEICR)]))
 646: row("latch rw one", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 1, "a write then a read at ONE offset announce once: the WRITE sets the bit the READ tests")
 647: t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([LD_BU(4, IEICR), LD_BU(4, IEICR)]))
 648: row("latch rd rd", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 1, "two READS at one offset announce once: a latch SET only on writes prints 2 (grok A) -- the setter x tester cell rw one cannot see")
 649: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([LD_BU(4, IEICR), ST_B(5, IEICR)]))
 650: row("latch rd wr", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 1, "a READ then a write at one offset announce once: the read's bit silences the write (the fourth setter x tester cell)")
 651: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, IEICR), ST_B(5, IEBERR), ST_B(5, IEICR)]))
 652: row("latch aba", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 2, "A,B,A announces TWICE: `|=' -> `=' forgets A when B announces and prints 3 (codex) -- earlier bits survive a new offset")
 653: # -- the fold and the text
 654: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, 0x6a)]))
 655: row("alias 0x6a", fate(alive, how), "survived", "0x6a folds onto 0x2a (`relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE'): the real part's fold too (VMESBCP2 Table 6-1, `Repeated')")
 656: row("alias msg", t.count("offset 0x2a") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...and the announce names the FOLDED offset 0x2a: a changed fold names another")
 657: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a") + program([ST_B(5, PRTICR)]))
 658: row("dir wr text", t.count("unimplemented write to") if alive else "n/a", 1, "the announce says WRITE for a store (an inverted writeflag test in the text says read)")
 659: row("wr declined", t.count("declined") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...and the word is DECLINED (no other row counts it: `ignored' would pass every count row)")
 660: row("wr val text", t.count("0x30 (len 1): 0x5a -- declined") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...and the announced VALUE is the byte written: the mirror of `srvd val text' on the write arm -- `idata -> 0' printed 0x0 and passed 36 rows (Opus M_WRVAL0, MEASURED)")
 661: t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([LD_BU(4, PRTICR)]))
 662: row("dir rd text", t.count("unimplemented read from") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...and READ for a load")
 663: # -- the served VALUE: a guest word store to the CASED T1CMP puts 0x34 in its interior byte 0x05, then an uncased ld.bu there
 664: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x12345678") + ["r7=0xaabb"] + program([ST_W(5, T1CMP), LD_BU(4, 0x05), LD_HU(6, 0x06), ST_H(7, 0x06), LD_HU(6, 0x06), LD_BU(8, 0x05), ST_B(7, 0x07), LD_BU(9, 0x07)]) + ["print r4", "print r6", "print r8", "print r9"])
 665: row("srvd val", val(t, alive, how), "0x34", "the served byte is the array's CURRENT byte, here one the guest wrote through the cased word arm -- not a default")
 666: row("srvd val text", t.count("0x5 (len 1) -- served 0x34") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...and the announce prints THAT byte (data[0], what was served) WITH its width: `served 0x%02x' of 0 passed 23 rows (Opus M_O1, MEASURED); `(len 1)' -> `(len 4)' passed 36 (codex)")
 667: row("srvd half text", t.count("0x6 (len 2) -- served 0x56") if alive else "n/a", 1, "a len-2 default read at 0x06 is announced from its FIRST byte (0x56 of 0x5678): data[len-1] says 0x78 (Opus M_LASTBYTE, MEASURED 36/36); a len-2 re-kill dies here")
 668: row("half wr rdawr", (printed(t, "r6") if alive else fate(alive, how)), "0x5678", "...then st.h 0xaabb at 0x06 is DECLINED (silent: the bit is set) and ld.hu reads 0x5678 back: len-2 disposition; a byte-truncated backed store reads 0xbb78")
 669: row("srvd rd latch", (printed(t, "r8") if alive else fate(alive, how)), "0x34", "a SECOND read at 0x05 -- its bit set by the FIRST READ -- still receives 0x34: a latched-path read whose response is zeroed passes count rows (codex M_LATCH_RD_ZERO)")
 670: row("srvd wr latch", (printed(t, "r9") if alive else fate(alive, how)), "0x78", "st.b 0xbb at 0x07 (declined, announced) then ld.bu at 0x07 -- its bit set by a WRITE -- receives 0x78: the response is preserved on the latched path whichever direction set the bit")
 671: # -- wide accesses at default offsets: the old kill was width-agnostic, so a `len != 1' re-kill is a partial reinstatement (grok B)
 672: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x5a5a5a5a") + program([ST_W(5, IEERR), LD_W(4, IEERR)]) + ["print r4"])
 673: row("wide wr", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "a guest st.w at IEERR (0x28..0x2b, all uncased) survives and is declined: reads back 0x0 (a memcpy-backed store reads 0x5a5a5a5a)")
 674: row("wide wr msg", t.count("offset 0x28 (len 4)") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...announced once, with the width; the ld.w at the same offset is silenced by the per-offset bit")
 675: row("wide wr val", t.count("(len 4): 0x5a5a5a5a -- declined") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...and the announced value is the WHOLE word: printing data[0] instead of idata says 0x5a (Opus M_WRDATA0, MEASURED 36/36)")
 676: t, alive, how = session(setup() + program([LD_W(4, PRTICR)]) + ["print r4"])
 677: row("wide rd", val(t, alive, how), "0x0", "a guest ld.w at PRTICR (0x30..0x33, all uncased) survives and is served the four zero bytes")
 678: row("wide rd srvd", t.count("(len 4) -- served 0x00") if alive else "n/a", 1, "...and the announce names the width and the first served byte")
 679: # -- the cased arms are untouched
 680: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x82") + program([ST_B(5, GENCTL), LD_BU(4, GENCTL)]) + ["print r4"])
 681: row("genctl rt", val(t, alive, how), "0x82", "an IMPLEMENTED byte register still round-trips (no C040 bit in 0x82)")
 682: t, alive, how = session(setup(r5="0x12345678") + program([ST_W(5, T1CMP), LD_W(4, T1CMP)]) + ["print r4"])
 683: row("t1cmp word rt", val(t, alive, how), "0x12345678", "the cased 32-bit counter arm still round-trips a word: the fix swallowed no cased arm")
 684: t, alive, how = session(["put b 0xfff4202a, 0x00"])
 685: row("put b ctl", fate(alive, how), "survived", "CONTROL: the debugger's put b never reaches a DM_DEFAULT arm (measured before the fix too)")
 686: row("put b ctl msg", t.count(MSG) if alive else "n/a", 0, "...so it prints nothing -- not a witness, a control")
 687: print("PCC2D_RESULT=%d/%d" % (len(rows) - len(bad), len(rows)))
 688: if bad:
 689:     print("PCC2D_RED=%s" % ",".join(bad))
 690: print("PCC2D_PROBE_DONE")
 691: 
 692: ```

 737: 
 738: ## THE CHANGELOG BLOCK (as it stands)
 739: 
 740: ```
 741: # Round #501 pcc2defaultkill -- the `default:` arm of DEVICE_ACCESS(pcc2) ended the host on any access to an uncased offset, reads included; now reads keep the served byte and writes are declined, one latched announce per offset
 742: 
 743: **2026-09-05.  HIGH, ranked first by the 2026-09-04 fix-review adjudication.  Product change: `src/devices/dev_pcc2.c`
 744: -- the `exit(1)` replaced by a per-offset latched `fatal()` (`uint64_t warned_unimpl`, one bit per byte offset) that
 745: says DECLINED for a write and SERVED 0x%02x for a read, then `break` to the handler's single `return 1`.  Detector:
 746: `regress/pcc2_default_probe.py`, 46 rows (23 at pass 2; +13 by the pass-2 repair, +10 by the pass-3 repair, below), run by gate 14.  Witness: rung 3, measured before the edit.**
 747: 
 748: ## The defect and its witness
 749: 
 750: Forty-three of the 0x40 byte offsets have no `case` (derived from `mvme_pcctworeg.h` against the 21 labels; two seats
 751: re-derived it): eighteen NAMED registers the model does not implement (PSCALECNT, GPIO_ICR/PCR, SCCMOIACK, SCCTXIACK,
 752: IEERR, IEICR, IEBERR, SCSIERR, the printer block, SPEED, PRTDATA), the twelve bytes INSIDE the four 32-bit
 753: counter/compare words, and thirteen unnamed offsets.  Any access to one of them -- a READ included, although the
 754: memcpy above the switch had already served it -- reached `default:` and `exit(1)`.
 755: 
 756: **The witness (`regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/witness_20260905/`), measured on the committed build BEFORE the
 757: edit: 7/13.**  A guest `st.b` to IEICR (0x2a) ends the host with exit status 1, the arm's own `pcc2: unimplemented`
 758: line printed first (the arm was reached); a guest `ld.bu` there likewise -- the READ side kills too; IEBERR and the
 759: in-word byte 0x05 and the alias 0x6a kill the same way.  Liveness (the probe's scratch round-trip) and
 760: device-signature (CHIPID reads PCC2_ID 0x20) controls pass.  **The debugger's `put b` does NOT reach the arm** -- it
 761: survives and prints nothing -- which the filing had called the cheapest witness (measured false; corrected).  The
 762: witness script is the width probe's own `session()` helper: a first draft hand-rolled the pty wait, accepted a
 763: STALE prompt, and read every kill as "survived" -- the carrier's rule about hand-rolled pty plumbing, re-learned.
 764: The combined IEBERR/IEERR row measures IEBERR only (the exiting build dies at the first); IEERR's kill follows from
 765: source (codex).  **13/13 on the fixed build.**
 766: 
 767: ## The design (pass 1: nine seats, `_scratchpad/panel_20260905_021751`, converged)
 768: 
 769: READ: keep the byte the memcpy served and SAY it is served -- it is the register array's CURRENT byte (inside a running
 770: COUNT word, the tick's; the pass-1 design said "default, not a computed value", corrected at pass 2), so this is not
 771: the option set's member 4 and a bounded announce about it is not a wrong record.  WRITE:
 772: member 3, DECLINE + latched announce -- NOT member 6 (backed accept), on three warrants the seats measured or read:
 773: the file's own cased siblings contradict a blanket store (SCCERR is write-to-clear -- the board manual's SCLR bits
 774: read as zero; the ICRs mask and reassert; PSCALECNT/SPEED/the IACKs are read-only); the addition licence is unmet
 775: (no inspected OpenBSD driver path reaches these offsets on this model -- the rung-3 witness is itself a guest, so the
 776: pass-1 wording "no guest reaches" was too wide: codex, pass 2); a store from the one arm 43 offsets reach adds a bounded-write
 777: surface into `pcctwo_reg[]` at 0x3d.  The Opus seat BUILT both candidates in a scratch clone and measured the
 778: 13-row witness at 13/13 under BOTH -- disposition-blind -- so the detector carries read-after-write rows in ONE
 779: session (`ieicr rdawr`, `ieerr rdawr`: store 0x5a then load, want 0x0 -- a backed accept reads 0x5a) with the CASED
 780: write-to-clear sibling `sccerr rdawr` as the control that reads 0x0 under either.
 781: 
 782: **Two premises of the pass-1 brief were wrong and are corrected here.**  The in-tree guest driver (`if_ie.c`) writes
 783: three of these offsets, but NEVER RUNS on this model: `machine_mvme88k.c:106` installs `unreadable` at the 82596
 784: address, `if_ie.c:407-409` fails its match on `badpaddr()` there, and `ie_setupram()` returns early at `:561-564`
 785: before the PCC2 store at `:609` (Fable, Opus, grok, codex independently; verified).  `ie0` IS configured in
 786: `conf/M187` and `conf/NFS` -- the brief's conf grep looked in the wrong files.  So the HIGH ranking rests on the
 787: witness and severity, not on the driver.  And the alias the model implements (`relative_addr %= PCC2_SIZE`) is the
 788: REAL part's: Motorola VMESBCP2 Table 6-1 lists `$FFF42040-$FFF42FFF  PCCchip2 Registers  Repeated` -- the codex seat
 789: cited it, the manual was fetched into `_scratchpad/` and the line read (`vmesbcp2.txt:4629`).  Two seats had rightly
 790: called the fold unestablished before the manual was on disk.
 791: 
 792: Recorded alternatives: the codex seat's ONE latch for the whole site, writes only (not adopted: a driver scanning
 793: registers should see each distinct one announced; both forms are bounded).  Corrections to the option set from this
 794: round: `return 0` denotes a FAILED TRANSACTION, not inherently unmapped hardware (an IACK cycle with no interrupt
 795: pending times out on the real part).  Disclosed: the in-word bytes are the interior of IMPLEMENTED counters, declined
 796: here as a width/alignment matter (`pcc2widecompose`), not claimed as unimplemented registers; the IACK offsets
 797: 0x23/0x25 are declined like the rest -- modelling them on the SCCRXIACK shape would open two new entries into the
 798: C040 path, so `pcc2c040kill` goes first (filed as a cross-round obligation); per-instance latch scope is not
 799: provable by two fresh sessions (each is a new process) -- a two-instance fixture is filed.
 800: 
 801: ## The detector as it shipped at PASS 2 (`regress/pcc2_default_probe.py`, 23 rows then; 46 now -- see the pass-2 and pass-3 sections; gate 14 pins all 46)
 802: 
 803: Controls: `live`, `devsig chipid`, `sccerr rdawr` (cased sibling), `genctl rt`, `t1cmp word rt` (the cased arms are
 804: untouched), `put b ctl` + `put b ctl msg` (the debugger path never reaches the arm).  Survival: `named wr`, `named rd`
 805: (all eighteen named offsets in ONE session per direction -- valid because the fix has no per-offset branch;
 806: `named wr msg` localises), `inword wr`, `unnamed wr`, `alias 0x6a`.  Disposition: `ieicr rdawr`, `ieerr rdawr` (0x0,
 807: not the stored byte), `named rd srvd` (the read announce says SERVED, never ignored).  Latch: `named wr msg` 18,
 808: `named rd msg` 18, `latch sameoff` 1, `latch twooff` 2, `latch rw one` 1.  Text: `alias msg` (the announce names the
 809: FOLDED offset), `dir wr text`, `dir rd text`.  The pty helper is the width probe's, extracted at run time -- the
 810: frozen instrument is therefore a PAIR (both files copied beside the table).
 811: 
 812: ## The kill table (`regress/artifacts/pcc2defaultkill/killtable_20260905/driver.log`; predictions in `PRETABLE_501.out`, mtime 04:12:20; run 1 launched 04:12:43, frozen default probe c76e053a; run 2 -- the table quoted -- frozen a96b8bcf, the status-last instrument gate 14 counts; width helper a080e2bc in both)
 813: 
 814: | run | predicted | measured | red rows |
 815: |---|---|---|---|
 816: | BASE (fresh clone of build/, rebuilt) | 23/23 | **23/23** | -- |
 817: | `M_EXIT` -- the verbatim `exit(1)` body reinstated | 8/23 | **7/23** | named wr, named wr msg, named rd, named rd msg, named rd srvd, inword wr, unnamed wr, ieicr rdawr, ieerr rdawr, latch sameoff, latch twooff, latch rw one, alias 0x6a, alias msg, dir wr text, dir rd text -- sixteen (the prediction listed fifteen and miscounted its own green set) |
 818: | `M_UNLATCH` -- `if (1)` for the latch test | 21/23 | **21/23** | latch sameoff 2, latch rw one 2 -- exact |
 819: | `M_ONELATCH` -- one whole-arm bit (`1ULL`) | 19/23 | **19/23** | named wr msg 1, named rd msg 1, named rd srvd 1, latch twooff 1 -- exact |
 820: | `M_BACKED` -- a store into `pcctwo_reg[]` added (member 6) | 21/23 | **21/23** | ieicr rdawr 0x5a, ieerr rdawr 0x5a -- exact; `sccerr rdawr` (the cased write-to-clear control) stayed 0x0 |
 821: | `M_RDKILL` -- `if (writeflag == MEM_READ) exit(1);` | 18/23 | **16/23** | named rd, named rd msg, named rd srvd, dir rd text, latch rw one (the read in that session exits) -- as predicted -- plus ieicr rdawr and ieerr rdawr, whose store-then-LOAD sessions exit at the load |
 822: | `M_DIRSWAP` -- the announce's direction test inverted | 20/23 | **20/23** | dir wr text 0, dir rd text 0, named rd srvd 0 -- exact |
 823: 
 824: Six of six killed; four predictions exact, two missed in the SAFE direction (more reds), each derived: M_EXIT's
 825: prediction ENUMERATED sixteen red rows -- the measured set exactly -- and CALLED them fifteen, so its derived score was
 826: 8/23 against a measured 7/23 (the first draft of this sentence said "listed fifteen"; the set was right and only the
 827: numeral and the derived score were wrong -- Opus, codex, kimi, pass 2) -- an arithmetic slip in the

 858: GO-WITH-CHANGES, provisional); three said NOT CLOSE (codex, grok, Opus).  **Count the arguments: the three carried

 861: the fold bounds the shift (`uint64_t`, `%= 0x40`), `break` reaches the one `return 1`, the latch words are separate
 862: fields both zeroed at DEVINIT, no cased arm is touched -- and, as Opus put it, the arm can only add output where the

 867: 
 868: | seat | mutant | why 23 rows could not see it |
 869: |---|---|---|
 870: | Opus (MEASURED 23/23) | the read announce's value `-> 0` (one token) | `named rd srvd` counts the PHRASE `served 0x`, never the value; no row served a nonzero byte |
 871: | Opus (MEASURED 23/23) | member 6 on the LATCHED path: `} else if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) pcctwo_reg[ra] = idata;` | both `rdawr` rows store ONCE to a fresh offset in a fresh process; the latch state was built by `sameoff`/`twooff`/`rw one` and observed on the COUNT channel only |
 872: | grok | the latch SET conditioned on writes | the bit-set cell was reached only through a WRITE (`rw one`); two reads at one offset had no row -- the setter's direction is a third axis the (writeflag x latch) 2x2 hides |
 873: | grok | `if (len != 1) exit(1);` -- the removed kill was width-agnostic, so this is a PARTIAL reinstatement | every default-path row was `st.b`/`ld.bu`; the pass-1 PRETABLE's own SIBLING PARITY sentence said "byte-and-wide" and no row drove wide |
 874: | codex | `|=` `->` `=` at the latch update (forgets earlier bits when a new offset announces) | A,A and A,B and W,R never REVISIT an offset after another has announced; A,B,A wants 2 and the mutant prints 3 |

 876: 
 877: **The diagnosis, from the pass-1 PRETABLE's own GUARD-FIELD line:** *"they disagree only in the latch's own state,
 878: which the sameoff/twooff/rw-one rows build"* -- true, and the state was observed on one channel (the announce count)
 879: while the mutant that matters (Opus M_O2) changes the OTHER channel (the read-back value) in exactly that state.  The
 880: guard/body check found the cell and the row was written against the wrong observable.  And codex found a product
 881: defect the rows could not: the read announce RELOADED `pcctwo_reg[relative_addr]` after the response was copied, and
 882: the SIGALRM tick writes back the two COUNT words (`dev_pcc2.c` `write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT]..` and T2COUNT)
 883: -- so for the six bytes inside those two words the announced byte could differ from the served one while a counter
 884: runs.  `"the array's default, NOT a computed value"` was therefore also wrong for those six.  **Pass 3 narrowed and
 885: settled this in three readings.**  grok: the tick only READS the compare words and CEN is off at init, so under this
 886: instrument -- `srvd val` lives in T1CMP's interior -- `data[0]` and a reload are the same byte, and `srvd val text` pins
 887: the constant-0 mutant, NOT the reload (Opus then MEASURED the reload mutant at a clean sweep; disclosed in the contract).
 888: Fable: the mid-handler race is unreachable because ticks run between guest instructions -- REFUTED by reading
 889: `src/core/timer.c`: `timer_tick(int signal_nr)` IS the SIGALRM handler and calls `timer->timer_tick(timer, timer->extra)`
 890: directly, and `dev_pcc2.c` registers `pcc_timer_tick` with `timer_add`, so the pcc2 tick is signal-driven and can land
 891: anywhere, the copy-to-announce gap included.  Fable's SECOND reason needs no timing and is added to the comment: on the
 892: #119 END-SPAN path the read is zero-filled while the array byte is kept, so a reload would announce a value the guest
 893: did not receive (Opus adds a third backstop: `memory_rw.c` `#95` pre-zeroes the buffer before device dispatch).
 894: `data[0]` is definitionally what was served -- the right operand under every reading.
 895: 
 896: **The repair (one diff, class closure -- the repair-chain amendment: consecutive cells of one class are closed
 897: together, not one per pass).**  PRODUCT: both announces carry `(len %d)`; the read announce prints `data[0]` -- the
 898: byte actually SERVED -- with a comment saying why.  DETECTOR, 36 rows (+13): `latch rd rd` and `latch rd wr` complete
 899: the setter x tester product (W,W `sameoff`; W,R `rw one`; R,R; R,W); `latch aba` pins accumulation across offsets;
 900: `sameoff val` reads back after the SECOND write (the latched path's disposition); `srvd val` + `srvd val text` serve
 901: a byte the GUEST wrote (a word store through the cased T1CMP arm puts 0x34 at 0x05) and pin the announced value;
 902: `wide wr` (+`msg`) and `wide rd` (+`srvd`) drive `st.w`/`ld.w` at uncased word-aligned offsets and pin the width
 903: text; `inword wr`/`unnamed wr` now drive all 12 / all 13, and `inword rd`/`unnamed rd` read them -- **every one of
 904: the 43 uncased offsets is written once and read once**; `wr declined` pins the word.  Row names stay under 14
 905: characters (the gate's `%-16s` anchor).  RECORDS (the L4s the seats named): the kill-table header's `c76e053a ==
 906: live` (run 1's md5 against run 2's table; the live probe was `a96b8bcf`); "three predictions exact" (four); the
 907: M_EXIT diagnosis (its 16-row red SET was right, only the numeral and the derived score were wrong); "would have
 908: shipped green and counted nothing" (the gate FAILED 25/844 and caught it); the `alias 0x6a` row saying "not proven
 909: of the real part" beside a docstring citing Table 6-1; `unnamed wr` describing an overrun M_BACKED's end-span guard
 910: prevents; "four arms up" (ten case blocks); "no guest reaches these offsets" (the witness IS a guest -- limited to
 911: the inspected OpenBSD driver paths); "unnamed" made explicit as "the fork header has no label" (the manual names
 912: 0x39 as SPEED's second byte and 0x3b as PRTDATA's low byte -- codex).  Pass-2 mini-packet findings adopted: the
 913: announce under-described a wide read (deepseek, grok, Opus, codex all named it) -- fixed by the width label.
 914: Rejected: minimax's "unsafe" label on the M_RDKILL prediction miss (a predicted-green row that went red is the
 915: SAFE direction, by the round's own rule).  Recorded, not adopted: codex's per-instance latch point is `pcc2latchinst`
 916: (filed); Opus noted there is NO mvme88k boot rig (`grep -l mvme regress/*.sh` is empty), so the two gate-14 probes are

1068: **The five safe misses are ONE class, recorded with their values:** the repair appended a re-read of 0x05 to the served-value
1069: session (`LD_BU(8, 0x05)`, the `srvd rd latch` row), so under any mutant that leaves 0x05 UNLATCHED at that point -- the
1070: latch disabled (M_UNLATCH), scoped to IEICR/IEERR (M_LATCHOFF), set only on writes (M_WRLATCH), or forgotten by `=`
1071: (M_ABA) -- the re-read announces a second `0x5 (len 1) -- served 0x34` and `srvd val text` counts 2 against a want of 1.

1073: in-word and unnamed WRITE rows, whose sessions now end in the read-back the mutant kills.  Every predicted red went red;
1074: no predicted red stayed green.  The table-3 section's late-recorded `latch sameoff 3` was the same family.

 209: 		    (old_count1 < compare1 && count1 < old_count1)) {

 215: 					interrupt1 ++;
 216: 				}

 218: 			}
 219: 		}

 225: 			d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1CTL] |= (oc << 4);
 226: 		}
 227: 	}

 260: 	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T1COUNT], count1);
 261: 	write32(&d->pcctwo_reg[PCCTWO_T2COUNT], count2);

 481:     def rd(t, until=None):
 482:         #  drain the pty for t seconds; with `until', return as soon as that text has
 483:         #  arrived (pass 7: a fixed drain after `continue' could send the next command to a
 484:         #  guest that had not reached its breakpoint yet).
 485:         nonlocal buf
 486:         end = time.time() + t
 487:         while time.time() < end:
 488:             if until is not None and until in buf:
 489:                 return True
 490:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 491:             if not r:
 492:                 continue
 493:             try:
 494:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 495:                 if not d:
 496:                     return False
 497:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 498:             except OSError:
 499:                 return False
 500:         if until is not None and until in buf:    # pass 9 (codex): the token may land in the last read
 501:             return True
 502:         return "timeout" if until is not None else True

 504:     alive = rd(settle)

 511:             c, w = c[0], c[1]
 512:         try:
 513:             #  the one unbounded call left (pass 9, Opus): it blocks only if the pty input buffer fills

CHANGELOG.md:12073:- **#269** `devices/dev_asc.c` (both trees): **#265** relocated the ASC FIFO overflow warning into the drop guard, which turned an at-most-once-per-fill message into **one host line per dropped byte** — measured, 24 overflow writes produced 24 host lines. The pre-existing read-side twin (the **#197** empty-FIFO guard, whose message says "overrun" for historical reasons although the condition is a read of an *empty* FIFO) behaves identically: 24 reads of an empty FIFO produced 24 lines. This matters beyond noise, because `fatal()` is **not verbosity-gated** and `va_debug()` writes stdout **one character at a time** — the same stream the boot harness pattern-matches — so an undrained pipe can block the single-threaded emulator. Both sites now warn **once per device**, via one-shot latches in `struct asc_data`, and deliberately keep `fatal()`: a verbosity-gated `debugmsg()` was rejected because under `-q` the harness verbosity settles at `VERBOSITY_ERROR` (`main.c` lowers it by one; `debugmsg_add_verbosity_level()` floors at 0), so a WARNING-level message would be **invisible to the boot-log hygiene grep**, while an ERROR-level one would change the printed form *and* would trip **#261**'s global break-on-ERROR whenever that is armed. The latches are deliberately **not** cleared by `dev_asc_reset()` or `dev_asc_fifo_flush()` — otherwise a guest could re-arm the flood with `RSTCHIP` — and are zeroed by the `memset` in `dev_asc_init()`. Everything the guest can observe (the dropped byte, the value returned by a read of an empty FIFO, the FIFO indices and count, the registers, the interrupts) is unchanged.
CHANGELOG.md:12095:Verified: build **0/0** both trees (223 pmax / 224 arc objects); **pmax 15/15 + arc 13/13 → `uid=0(root)`**; log-hygiene greps **0** on both boot pty logs. **Measured first, on the committed build** (`p2_fp_cvt.py`): **11 of 13** non-control cases diverged from the hardware default, **identically on arc/R4000 and pmax/R3000A** ("arc vs pmax differences: none") — NaN, ±Inf and ±1e30 all returned `0x00000000` and +2^31 returned `0x80000000`. **#273 probe, 55/55 on arc and 44/44 on pmax**, across **all three** instructions that reach this arm (`cvt.w.fmt`, `trunc.w.fmt`, `trunc.l.fmt` — all route through `FPU_OP_CVT`; there is **no `cvt.l`** in the tree, so `IEEE_FMT_L` is produced by `trunc.l` alone): every invalid case now returns the pinned constant and every in-range control is unchanged (`2147483647.0`→`0x7fffffff`, `-2^31`→`0x80000000`, `3.0`/`3.5`/`3.25`→`3`, `-3.25`→`0xfffffffd`). The asymmetry is covered by its own boundary cases: **`-2147483648.5` → `0x80000000`** (the value a `< -2147483648.0` predicate would have wrongly saturated) and, for L, **−2^63 → `0x8000000000000000`** while **−2^63−2048 → `0x7fffffffffffffff`**. **In-guest end-to-end on OpenBSD/pmax**, against a purpose-built pre-#273 binary: `awk 'BEGIN{print sprintf("%d", 1e30)}'` prints **0 before → 2147483647 after**, and `-1e30` likewise prints **+2147483647**, the sign-independence no software clamp would reproduce; the in-range controls (`2147483647`, `-2147483648`, `3.9`→`3`, `-3.9`→`-3`) are byte-identical on both binaries. Note that awk's **`int()`** does *not* exercise this path — it stays in floating point (`int(1e30)` prints `1.00000000000000001988462483866e+30` on both binaries, and `int(3.9)` yields `4`, i.e. it is not a truncating C cast) — so `printf`/`sprintf` `%d`, which does cast a double to a C `long`, is the route that reaches the FPU.

The detector still admits undisclosed changes to the announcement’s value and halfword-width channels. These are derived escapes, not rebuilt-mutant measurements: this workspace is read-only.

1. **The value repair does not observe the low bit on either arm.** In the WRITE announcement, replace `(int) idata` with `(int) idata & ~1`. Both `wr val text` and `wide wr val` remain green: their asserted values, `0x5a` and `0x5a5a5a5a`, are even. The newly appended byte write announces `0xbb`, which becomes incorrectly printed as `0xba`, but `srvd wr latch` checks the subsequent read response—not that announcement. Every other row observes an unaffected channel. **Predicted result: 46/46.**

   Its READ mirror also survives: replace `data[0]` with `data[0] & ~1` in the READ announcement. Every exact served-value anchor expects an even value: `0x00`, `0x34`, or `0x56`. **Predicted result: 46/46.** These are unconditional formatting-operand changes within the claimed value channel; the stated exclusions do not cover them. [Announcement operands](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_pcc2.c:692), [value assertions](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/pcc2_default_probe.py:190).

2. **The halfword WRITE announcement is never exercised.** The sole `ST_H` follows an `LD_HU` at the same offset, so its latch is already set. Replacing the WRITE formatting operand `(int) len` with `(int) len & ~2` preserves the checked widths 1 and 4 while misreporting width 2 as 0. `half wr rdawr` remains green because neither storage nor the read response changes; the defective announcement is suppressed. **Predicted result: 46/46.** The statement that the len-2 disclosure is “CLOSED” therefore needs a narrower scope or another observation. [Halfword session](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/pcc2_default_probe.py:194).

The repair’s instruction mechanics check out. The decoder confirms `LD_HU = 0x02` and `ST_H = 0x0a`; the new accesses are aligned. The final register values are correctly `r4=0x34`, `r6=0x5678`, `r8=0x34`, and `r9=0x78`. The byte write at `0x07` uses `0xbb`, is declined, and leaves the seeded `0x78` intact. The halfword READ text anchor cannot match the WRITE format: `-- served` and `: value -- declined` distinguish them. However, the first halfword response in `r6` is overwritten before inspection; its announcement is observed, its complete returned value is not independently observed. [Decoder](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_m88k_instr.c:3045).

For the ten new rows, the mirror audit is:

| New row | Mirror cell and disposition |
|---|---|
| `wr val text` | READ byte announcement: **ROWED** by `srvd val text`. WRITE word announcement: **ROWED** by `wide wr val`. WRITE halfword announcement: **NEITHER**. |
| `wide wr val` | READ word announcement: **ROWED** by `wide rd srvd`, but its all-zero bytes cannot distinguish byte selection. WRITE halfword value: **NEITHER**. |
| `srvd half text` | First WRITE halfword announcement, including width and value: **NEITHER**. Byte and word READ announcements: **ROWED**. |
| `half wr rdawr` | Halfword WRITE with a clear latch, or with a latch set by a preceding WRITE: **NEITHER**. The existing row exercises a latch set by a READ. |
| `srvd rd latch` | Byte READ after a WRITE set the latch: **ROWED** by `srvd wr latch`. Halfword READ after a READ set it: **ROWED** by the final `r6` observation. |
| `srvd wr latch` | Byte READ after a READ set the latch: **ROWED** by `srvd rd latch`. Halfword READ after a WRITE first set it: **NEITHER**. |
| `inword msg` | Named and unnamed WRITE-set count cells: **ROWED**. In-word READ-set replay is observed through `srvd val text`/`srvd half text`; `inword rd` itself checks only survival. |
| `unnamed msg` | Named and in-word WRITE-set count cells: **ROWED**. Unnamed READ-set replay and first-READ announcement count: **NEITHER**; `unnamed rd` checks survival only. |
| `inword rdawr` | Named and unnamed disposition cells: **ROWED**. Byte WRITE disposition after a READ set the latch: **NEITHER**; `latch rd wr` checks count without subsequent value inspection. |
| `unnamed rdawr` | Named and in-word disposition cells: **ROWED**. The unnamed READ-set/WRITE/read-back mirror: **NEITHER**. |

Single-offset sampling, len 8, and per-instance scope remain **DISCLOSED** exclusions. They do not establish coverage of the missing setter-direction or halfword-announcement cells.
